Guidance

C606 questions
C-6858C-7066C-7162C-7264C-8562C-7350

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Guidance is credible, specific on costs and synergies, and conservative on cash; consensus sits exactly on it for FY27 (5.0% margin, £150m free cash flow) and adds only the guided synergies for FY28. Mitie's record is to set floors and beat them (seven consensus upgrades after the 2023 Capital Markets Event; FY24 EBIT £20m above guidance; FY26 free cash flow 35% above guidance), and to raise adjacent ambitions rather than headline targets. My indicative FY27 bridge (about £290m to £305m of EBIT against a £300m floor) says the margin floor is attainable with modest headroom rather than deeply sandbagged, because the Department for Work and Pensions loss and continuing inflation absorb much of the synergy and telecoms gains; free cash flow guidance is the more conservative element. For Set 2: lean FY27 slightly above consensus on margin (5.0% to 5.1%) and above on free cash flow (£150m to £165m); treat FY28 as consensus-like (about 5.3%) with downside from any new statutory cost and upside from synergies beyond £30m; remember that guidance will lapse with the listing.

C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C58

Judgement. For FY27 (year to March 2027) consensus sits exactly on guidance: management guides an operating margin of at least 5% and free cash flow of £150m; the three brokers' median is EBIT £301m on revenue £6,000m (5.0%) and free cash flow £150m (Jefferies' EBITA £303m, Deutsche Bank's £300m; the FY27 EBITA consensus cited at the Q1 update was £302m). For FY28 there is no formal guidance (the strategic plan ends in FY27); the only guided element is the £30m Marlowe synergy target, and consensus (EBIT £331m, 5.3%) builds that in.

Why consensus does not sit above guidance despite the beat record. (1) All three brokers now rate the shares Hold and anchor on the offer; with the price fixed by the bid there is little reason to upgrade. (2) Coverage is thin (three brokers; Peel Hunt and Stifel published nothing in the Q1 window). (3) The margin has been flat for two years, which makes brokers cautious about a step to 5%. (4) Under the UK Takeover Code, profit forecasts made during an offer period carry reporting obligations, which discourages new formal guidance.

Reading. Given a management that habitually sets floors and beats them, guidance at "at least 5%" is a floor; consensus therefore embeds little or no beat. The free cash flow target of £150m is below the FY26 outturn of £162m and looks conservative.

C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

C66

Judgement. The record is consistent beat-and-quietly-raise rather than headline upgrades. Mitie produced seven consecutive upgrades to consensus profit after the October 2023 Capital Markets Event; beat FY24 EBIT guidance of £190m by £20m; raised the cost-saving target to £25m; beat FY26 free cash flow guidance (at least £120m) by 35%; delivered the three-year revenue target in two years; and raised the projects ambition (to £2bn) and compliance ambition (to £1.5bn). But it never raised the headline margin target (at least 5% by FY27) and slightly missed its FY26 revenue guidance (£5,619m against about £5,650m). The behaviour fits the Wisdom Library's "sandbagging precision" archetype for cash and profit (set conservative floors, deliver above them) and an honest-tracker archetype for revenue.

C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

C62

Judgement. FY27 margin guidance is moderately conservative and the free cash flow target clearly conservative, once adjusted for change forces that are more favourable in FY27 than in FY25-FY26.

History. Operating profit before other items rose from about £210m (FY24) to £234m (FY25, +11%) to £264m (FY26, +13%), with the margin flat at 4.6% to 4.7% because savings absorbed an exceptional statutory cost shock (FY26 gross hurdle £170m, £18.6m unrecovered).

FY27 change forces, an indicative EBIT bridge from £264m (my estimates from the files; Set 2 should refine).

  • Full-year consolidation of Marlowe (four extra months; Marlowe revenue perhaps £350m at about 6.6%): +£8m to £10m.
  • Incremental Marlowe synergies (£7m realised in FY26 toward a £15m run-rate in FY27): +£6m to £8m.
  • Concluded loss-making telecoms contract not recurring: about +£10m.
  • Department for Work and Pensions demobilisation: -£15m (management and Deutsche Bank).
  • Net contract wins and scope increases excluding that contract: about +£17m (Deutsche Bank).
  • Net inflation after pass-through: -£10m to -£12m (management guide, against -£18.6m before savings in FY26).
  • New margin enhancement savings: +£10m to £15m (on the FY26 run-rate of £25m gross).
  • Small bolt-ons: +£2m to £3m.
  • Total: about £290m to £305m, against guidance of at least £300m (5% of about £6,000m) and consensus £301m.

Reading. The bridge says the 5% floor is attainable with modest headroom; it is not deeply sandbagged, because the Department for Work and Pensions loss and continuing inflation absorb much of the synergy and telecoms gains. The FY27 outturn most likely lands at 5.0% to 5.1% (Jefferies forecasts 5.1%), unless Project PRIO costs fall inside the adjusted line (the files suggest they sit below it). For FY28, consensus (5.3%) relies on the remaining £15m of synergies, mix and no new statutory shock; that is reasonable but less conservative. Free cash flow: £150m against £162m in FY26 and rising EBIT is conservative even after PRIO and projects working capital.

C-72

How specific is the guidance: built from stated building blocks, or vague about how results come about?

C64

Judgement. Specific on the cost and synergy building blocks, vague on revenue composition and segment margins. Stated building blocks: the inflation bridge (gross inflation and National Insurance, recovery rates, net residuals), FY27 net inflation £10m to £12m including about £2m of fuel, PRIO £20m to £25m, Marlowe synergies £15m (FY27) and £30m (FY28), the Department for Work and Pensions headwind £15m, the telecoms recovery about £10m, the margin floor, free cash flow £150m, the leverage range 0.75x to 1.5x, and the pipeline conversion (over 70% of £32.5bn to be awarded within 18 months). Vague: no revenue guidance beyond a plan-level CAGR; no split of organic growth between price and volume except in passing (2% price in Q1 FY27); no segment margin guidance; no contract-level attrition data; whether PRIO and restructuring sit above or below the adjusted line is not stated clearly.

C-85

To what extent is informal or qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C62

Question asked To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement. Informal commentary is somewhat above formal guidance and consensus. Bentley expressed "absolute confidence" in the FY25-FY27 targets and spoke of building the platform for FY28 and beyond; management highlighted Technical Services having secured 70% of its FY27 sales target, a ten-fold rise in the compliance cross-sell pipeline to about £700m, and over 70% of the record pipeline to be awarded within 18 months; Deutsche Bank describes the £30m synergy target as "highly conservative"; management raised ambitions for projects (£2bn) and compliance (£1.5bn) and said "water is the new energy". Why: management had every incentive at the AGM of 21-Jul-2026, held on the day the offer was announced, to show strong standalone momentum, both to justify the board's view of value and to support completion. The tone points to FY27 at or above 5% and continued margin progression in FY28, slightly above consensus's 5.0% and 5.3%, with the caveat that informal optimism on cross-sell has so far run ahead of revenue.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

C50

Judgement. Four points.

  1. Guidance is now of limited relevance to listed holders. With completion expected in calendar Q1 2027, the H1 FY27 results on 19-Nov-2026 are probably the last major standalone print; formal guidance during an offer period is constrained by Takeover Code rules.
  2. Heavy reliance on adjusted measures. Mitie's other items (£113m) and the gap between adjusted and basic EPS (106%) are among the largest in the industry; the Industry Digest advises always using adjusted figures with the gap stated.
  3. Thin, homogeneous coverage. Three brokers in FactSet, all at Hold with targets of 218.5p to 221p (mean 219p); the buy share fell from 89% before the bid to 0%; coverage is concentrated in Jefferies and Deutsche Bank.
  4. Investor relations continuity. Kate Heseltine (Group Investor Relations and Corporate Finance Director) remains the contact through the transaction; management disclosure is clear on cost bridges but limited on contract-level data.

Sell-side estimates

C553 questions

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Consensus (three brokers, all Hold, anchored on the offer) is reasonable and close to my reading of the foundations: revenue growth slowing to about 5% as acquisitions stop, the margin rising from 4.7% to 5.0%, 5.3% and 5.4%, EPS growth accelerating to +15% and +14% in FY27-FY28, interest about £30m to £34m, tax about 25% plus minorities, a flat share count and free cash flow conversion of about 80% to 85%. C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

C58

Question asked How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

Judgement. Consensus forecasts a business that grows more slowly than in the past (because acquisitions stop) but converts revenue into earnings better than in the past (because the margin finally rises). The revenue gap is credible; the margin gap is credible for FY27, reasonably credible for FY28, and less certain for FY29; the EPS acceleration is credible only if the margin delivers; free cash flow is conservative.

Line by line (history FY24-FY26; consensus FY27-FY29).

  • Revenue growth: +12.7%, +14.3%, +10.5% (acquisitions included; organic about 5% to 8%) against +6.8%, +4.8%, +4.7%. FY27 is organic of about 4% plus about 3 points of acquisition carry-over; FY28-FY29 are organic only and slightly above the current 4% run-rate. Credible given order intake (+33%) and renewals (91%).
  • Margin (company operating margin before other items): 4.7%, 4.6%, 4.7% against 5.0%, 5.3%, 5.4%. Brokers' EBITDA margin 6.4% (FY26) against 6.7%, 6.9%, 6.9%. About 40 basis points is identifiable (Marlowe synergies to £30m, telecoms); the rest needs mix and savings to outrun inflation and client sharing. Credible to FY28; FY29 meets the Public Interest Test renewals.
  • EBIT: £264m (company FY26) against £301m, £331m, £353m (+14%, +10%, +7%), against +11% and +13% in FY25-FY26.
  • Depreciation and amortisation on the brokers' basis about £100m to £105m a year (EBITDA less EBIT), excluding amortisation of acquired intangibles; FactSet's history shows £137m including it. Do not mix the two.
  • Net finance cost: £16.2m (FY25), £27.7m (FY26) against an implied £34m, £31m, £29m. Plausible (full year of Marlowe debt; no buyback).
  • Tax: reported 25.4% and 26.9% in FY25-FY26; consensus pre-tax £267m to net income £190m implies about 29% including minorities (about 25% tax plus about £10m of minorities).
  • Shares: consensus about 1.30bn to 1.31bn flat (no buyback), against 1,202m (FY25) and 1,255m (FY26) on FactSet and 1,301.7m voting rights in September 2026.
  • EPS (brokers' basis): 11.3p, 11.8p, 12.6p (+4%, +7%) against 14.5p, 16.6p, 17.9p (+15%, +14%, +8%).
  • Free cash flow: £162m (FY26) against £150m, £178m, £198m; conversion of adjusted net income about 79%, 83%, 85% against about 100% in FY26. Conservative for FY27.
  • Capital expenditure: £33m to £40m (FY26) against £35m to £38m. Dividend per share 4.5p against 4.8p, 5.4p, 5.7p (about a 33% payout).
  • Total debt (FactSet, including leases) about £551m, £539m, £526m; shareholders' equity £438m, £559m, £677m.

Credibility given the foundations. The forecasts match the change forces found above (fading cost shock, synergies, steady demand, no buyback). The main omissions are recurring other items (consensus is adjusted), the public renewal risk from FY28, and the absence of any break-case leadership discount, which is appropriate for consensus but not for Set 2's downside case.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

C70

Judgement. A strong record of meeting or beating on profit and cash, with small misses on revenue and on interest.

  • Last year (FY26 and Q1 FY27). EBIT £264m: in line with Jefferies (£264m), slightly above Deutsche Bank (£262m). Margin 4.7% against 4.6% expected: beat. Adjusted pre-tax profit £236.4m against £233.5m: beat. EPS: about a 3% beat like for like (12.6p against Deutsche Bank's 12.2p); the headline "13.6p against 12.3p, a 10% beat" mixes bases. Free cash flow £162m against at least £120m guided: a 35% beat. Revenue £5,619m against £5,653m consensus and about £5,650m guidance: slight miss. Net interest £27.7m against Jefferies' £20m: a 38% miss. Q1 FY27 organic growth 4%: in line with Deutsche Bank, slightly below Jefferies (5%).
  • Last three years. Seven consecutive consensus profit upgrades after October 2023; FY24 EBIT £20m above £190m guidance; every target of the FY22-FY24 plan met (margin 4.7% in FY24); the Change Forces report records a 100% consensus beat rate over three years.
  • Last five years. FY21-FY22 were pandemic-distorted (Interserve integration, surge contracts); the record from FY22 onward is of delivery. Before 2017 (pre-Bentley) the company issued multiple profit warnings, which is not representative of current management.
  • Comparable environments. In the high-inflation, high statutory cost years FY24-FY26, Mitie held margin and beat on cash, i.e. it delivered in a tougher-than-normal environment.
C-74

To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

❌ D45

Judgement. No. Estimates have not been cut; they have drifted up. FY27 consensus EPS revisions were +1% over one month and +2% over three, six and twelve months; EBITDA revisions 0% to +1%; sales revisions 0% to +1%; target prices +3%, +8% and +10% over one, three and six months (toward the offer). The dashboard pins sell-side earnings momentum at C (revision-momentum score 1). The collapse of the buy share from 89% to 0% is a response to the bid, not to fundamentals. Guidance has not been rebased lower; the only "kitchen-sink" element is the large other items charge, which flatters future adjusted earnings rather than lowering the base. There is therefore no washed-out, rebased setup of the kind that precedes upgrade cycles; the stock is a bid situation, and in a break the estimates would face modest downgrade risk (leadership gap, PRIO costs, public renewals) rather than offering a low base.

Stock market narrative

❌ D451 question
C-2245

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The narrative has moved from "standalone compounder with a succession question" to "cash exit pending regulatory clearance". For listed holders the remaining staircase has one step, completion at 218.5p, well-founded and almost fully priced (spread 3.7%). The Competition and Markets Authority decision is the one dated event that can move the price materially, mostly by closing the spread or, in a low-probability outcome, by breaking the deal. In a break case the narrative would need rebuilding under new leadership, with credible but unexciting steps (5% margin, £150m free cash flow, buyback restart, synergies) against an approaching insourcing headwind; it would not support a re-rating above the stock's historical median quickly.

C-22

What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

❌ D45

Judgement. The staircase is short and its top step is fixed: for a listed holder the main positive development ahead is completion of the OCS offer at 218.5p, which is well-founded but not novel (it is expected and priced, apart from a 3.7% spread). Beyond completion there is no listed equity, so the 6-to-36-month steps exist only in a break case, where they would have to rebuild a standalone story from a lower base.

Next 6 months (to April 2027).

  • Competition and Markets Authority Phase 1 decision (not dated in the files; the latest Earnings Review expected it in late Q3 or early Q4 2026): clearance would remove the main risk and close most of the spread. Well-founded (Deutsche Bank: combined UK share below 20%); novelty low.
  • H1 FY27 results, 19-Nov-2026: likely confirmation of 4% organic growth, synergies on track and the 5% FY27 margin; well-founded, low novelty, little price effect while the bid stands.
  • Court sanction and completion, calendar Q1 2027: cash to holders.
  • Risk events, not steps: the Autumn Budget (November 2026) and any Phase 2 referral.

Months 6 to 18 (break case only). FY27 results (about June 2027) showing at least 5% margin and £150m free cash flow (well-founded); appointment of a new chief executive (novel, uncertain); restart of the £100m buyback (well-founded given leverage); a new strategic plan for FY28-FY30 (novel); first renewals under the Public Interest Test from April 2027 (a negative step).

Months 18 to 36 (break case only). Marlowe synergies reaching £30m in FY28 (well-founded); compliance toward £1bn to £1.5bn and projects toward £2bn of revenue (moderately founded); margin toward 5.5% (moderately founded); against that, post-2028 public renewals under the Public Interest Test (the main negative).
